Here is the complete, thorough report of my findings.

---

## Part 1: Existing Jinja2 Template Files

The prompts directory is at:
`/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/medgemma-hackathon/services/protocol-processor-service/src/protocol_processor/prompts/`

It contains **6 Jinja2 template files** (all `.jinja2` extension; no `.j2` or `.jinja` files exist):

| # | Template File | Purpose |
|---|--------------|---------|
| 1 | `system.jinja2` | System prompt for Gemini criteria extraction (role, rules, splitting, decomposition guidance, examples, assertion status, output format) |
| 2 | `user.jinja2` | User prompt for Gemini criteria extraction (takes `{{ title }}` variable) |
| 3 | `entity_decompose.jinja2` | Prompt for decomposing criterion text into discrete medical entities (takes `{{ criterion_text }}`, `{{ category }}`) |
| 4 | `grounding_system.jinja2` | System prompt for MedGemma grounding -- medical terminology expert role and confidence scoring rules |
| 5 | `grounding_evaluate.jinja2` | User prompt for MedGemma to select best terminology match from candidates (takes `{{ entity_text }}`, `{{ entity_type }}`, `{{ criterion_context }}`, `{{ candidates }}`) |
| 6 | `grounding_reasoning.jinja2` | User prompt for MedGemma agentic reasoning loop -- 3 retry questions (takes `{{ entity_text }}`, `{{ entity_type }}`, `{{ criterion_context }}`, `{{ previous_query }}`, `{{ attempt }}`) |

The `prompts/__init__.py` provides helper functions `get_prompts_env()`, `render_system_prompt()`, and `render_user_prompt()`.

---

## Part 2: Hardcoded Prompts Found in Python Source Files

### Finding 1: `tools/field_mapper.py` -- Lines 169-213

**File:** `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/medgemma-hackathon/services/protocol-processor-service/src/protocol_processor/tools/field_mapper.py`

**Lines:** 169-213

**Description:** Large inline prompt that instructs Gemini to decompose a criterion into Entity-Relation-Value-Unit field mappings (AutoCriteria pattern). It includes XML-tagged sections (`<role>`, `<entity_context>`, `<rules>`, `<anti_patterns>`) with detailed rules about relation operators, boolean normalization, temporal durations, relative value resolution, negation patterns, and anti-patterns.

**Corresponding Jinja2 template:** NONE. This is entirely hardcoded as a Python string concatenation in the `generate_field_mappings()` function.

**Prompt snippet (first few lines):**
```python
prompt = (
    "<role>Clinical trial protocol analyst. Decompose criterion"
    " into Entity-Relation-Value-Unit field mappings.</role>\n\n"
    "<entity_context>\n"
    f"Medical entity: {grounded_term} {code_context}\n"
    f"Criterion text: {criterion_text}\n"
    "</entity_context>\n\n"
    "<rules>\n"
    ...
```

---

### Finding 2: `tools/medgemma_decider.py` -- Lines 169-173

**File:** `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/medgemma-hackathon/services/protocol-processor-service/src/protocol_processor/tools/medgemma_decider.py`

**Lines:** 169-173

**Description:** Inline prompt used by `_structure_decision_with_gemini()` to instruct Gemini to parse MedGemma's free-text grounding analysis into a structured `GroundingDecision` object (code, system, preferred term, confidence, reasoning).

**Corresponding Jinja2 template:** NONE. It is a small f-string prompt.

**Prompt text:**
```python
prompt = (
    "Extract the grounding decision from this medical terminology analysis."
    " Return the selected code, system, preferred term, confidence,"
    f" and reasoning.\n\n{raw_text}"
)
```

---

### Finding 3: `tools/medgemma_decider.py` -- Lines 338-345

**File:** `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/medgemma-hackathon/services/protocol-processor-service/src/protocol_processor/tools/medgemma_decider.py`

**Lines:** 338-345

**Description:** Inline prompt used by `_structure_reasoning_with_gemini()` to instruct Gemini to parse MedGemma's free-text agentic reasoning into a structured `AgenticReasoningResult` (should_skip, is_derived, derived_term, rephrased_query, gemini_suggestion).

**Corresponding Jinja2 template:** NONE. It is an inline f-string.

**Prompt text:**
```python
prompt = (
    "Extract the structured reasoning from this medical entity analysis. "
    "Determine: (1) should_skip if entity is not a valid medical criterion, "
    "(2) is_derived and derived_term if entity maps to a standard concept, "
    "(3) rephrased_query if a better medical term would improve search. "
    "You may also add a gemini_suggestion with your own reformulation if "
    "you can improve on the analysis.\n\n"
    f"{raw_text}"
)
```

---

### Finding 4: `tools/entity_decomposer.py` -- Lines 215-228

**File:** `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/medgemma-hackathon/services/protocol-processor-service/src/protocol_processor/tools/entity_decomposer.py`

**Lines:** 215-228

**Description:** Inline system and user messages for the MedGemma fallback path in `medgemma_decompose_entities()`. When Gemini decomposition fails, this sends a prompt to MedGemma asking it to identify medical concepts for terminology coding (UMLS, SNOMED, ICD-10).

**Corresponding Jinja2 template:** NONE. The primary Gemini decomposition path correctly uses the `entity_decompose.jinja2` template (line 76), but this MedGemma fallback path hardcodes its own prompts.

**Prompt text:**
```python
SystemMessage(
    content=(
        "You are a medical terminology expert. Given a clinical trial "
        "eligibility criterion, list every discrete medical concept that "
        "should be coded in a terminology system (UMLS, SNOMED, ICD-10). "
        "For each concept, state the concept name and whether it is a "
        "Condition, Medication, Lab_Value, Procedure, Demographic, or "
        "Other."
    )
),
HumanMessage(
    content=f'Criterion: "{normalized_text}"\n\nList the medical entities.'
),
```

---

### Finding 5: `tools/entity_decomposer.py` -- Lines 244-248

**File:** `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/medgemma-hackathon/services/protocol-processor-service/src/protocol_processor/tools/entity_decomposer.py`

**Lines:** 244-248

**Description:** Inline prompt used by `medgemma_decompose_entities()` to instruct Gemini to structure MedGemma's free-text entity analysis into a typed `DecomposedEntityList`. This is a Gemini-structuring-MedGemma-output prompt.

**Corresponding Jinja2 template:** NONE.

**Prompt text:**
```python
structure_prompt = (
    "Extract the medical entities from this analysis into a structured list. "
    "Each entity needs a 'text' (the medical concept name) and 'entity_type' "
    "(one of: Condition, Medication, Lab_Value, Procedure, Demographic, Other)."
    f"\n\nAnalysis:\n{raw_text}"
)
```

---

### Finding 6: `tools/structure_builder.py` -- Lines 142-158

**File:** `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/medgemma-hackathon/services/protocol-processor-service/src/protocol_processor/tools/structure_builder.py`

**Lines:** 142-158

**Description:** Inline prompt in `detect_logic_structure()` that instructs Gemini to analyze the logical structure (AND/OR/NOT) of a criterion with multiple field mappings. Includes rules for tree construction and index references.

**Corresponding Jinja2 template:** NONE.

**Prompt text:**
```python
prompt = (
    "You are a clinical trial protocol analyst. Analyze the logical"
    " structure of this eligibility criterion.\n\n"
    f"Criterion text: {criterion_text}\n\n"
    f"Field mappings (indexed):\n{mappings_text}\n\n"
    "Instructions:\n"
    "- Determine how the field mappings are logically connected\n"
    "- AND = all conditions required simultaneously\n"
    "- OR = any one condition suffices\n"
    "- NOT = negation of a condition\n"
    "- ATOMIC = leaf node referencing a single"
    " field_mapping by index\n"
    "- Return a tree where the root is AND, OR, NOT, or ATOMIC\n"
    "- Every ATOMIC node must have field_mapping_index set to a"
    " valid index (0 to "
    f"{len(field_mappings) - 1})\n"
    "- Each field_mapping index should appear exactly once\n"
)
```

---

### Finding 7: `tools/ordinal_resolver.py` -- Lines 62-82

**File:** `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/medgemma-hackathon/services/protocol-processor-service/src/protocol_processor/tools/ordinal_resolver.py`

**Lines:** 62-82

**Description:** Inline prompt in `resolve_ordinal_candidates()` that instructs Gemini to identify clinical ordinal scoring systems (ECOG, Karnofsky, NYHA, Child-Pugh, GCS, etc.) from a list of candidate entities. Includes characteristics of ordinal systems and what to propose.

**Corresponding Jinja2 template:** NONE.

**Prompt text:**
```python
prompt = (
    "You are a clinical terminology expert specializing in medical scoring "
    "systems and ordinal scales.\n\n"
    "For each entity below, determine if it is a clinical ordinal scoring "
    "system (e.g. ECOG, Karnofsky, NYHA, Child-Pugh, GCS, APACHE II, MELD, "
    "mRS, SOFA, etc.).\n\n"
    "An ordinal scoring system has these characteristics:\n"
    ...
    f"Entities to evaluate:\n{entities_text}\n\n"
    "For each entity that IS an ordinal scale, propose:\n"
    "- scale_name: snake_case identifier for YAML config\n"
    "- entity_aliases: alternative names clinicians might use\n"
    "- loinc_code: LOINC code if known\n"
    "- values: list of grades with descriptions\n"
)
```

## Part 3: Files Verified as Clean (No Hardcoded Prompts)

The following files were checked and contain **no hardcoded LLM prompts**:

| File | Notes |
|------|-------|
| `tools/gemini_extractor.py` | Uses `render_prompts()` to load `system.jinja2` and `user.jinja2` -- CLEAN |
| `tools/gemini_utils.py` | Utility for creating structured LLM clients -- no prompts |
| `tools/omop_mapper.py` | Pure DB lookup, no LLM calls |
| `tools/unit_normalizer.py` | Pure DB lookup, no LLM calls |

## 1. The `prompts/__init__.py` Helper Functions

**File:** `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/medgemma-hackathon/services/protocol-processor-service/src/protocol_processor/prompts/__init__.py`

```python
from pathlib import Path
from jinja2 import Environment, FileSystemLoader

PROMPTS_DIR = Path(__file__).parent

def get_prompts_env() -> Environment:
    return Environment(
        loader=FileSystemLoader(str(PROMPTS_DIR)),
        autoescape=False,
    )

def render_system_prompt(**kwargs: object) -> str:
    env = get_prompts_env()
    template = env.get_template("system.jinja2")
    return template.render(**kwargs)

def render_user_prompt(**kwargs: object) -> str:
    env = get_prompts_env()
    template = env.get_template("user.jinja2")
    return template.render(**kwargs)
```

Key details:
- `PROMPTS_DIR` is the package directory itself (`.../prompts/`).
- `get_prompts_env()` creates a **new** `Environment` on every call (no caching/singleton).
- `render_system_prompt` and `render_user_prompt` are convenience wrappers that hardcode the template names `system.jinja2` and `user.jinja2`.
- `autoescape=False` -- appropriate since these are plain-text prompts, not HTML.

### 2a. `system.jinja2`

**File:** `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/medgemma-hackathon/services/protocol-processor-service/src/protocol_processor/prompts/system.jinja2`

This is the main criteria extraction system prompt. It uses XML-style section tags (`<role>`, `<rules>`, `<splitting>`, `<decomposition>`, `<examples>`, `<assertion_status>`, `<output_format>`). It contains zero Jinja2 variables -- it is a static template with no `{{ }}` or `{% %}` blocks. Full content is 235 lines covering:

Extract all inclusion and exclusion criteria from this clinical trial protocol PDF.

## 3. How Each Template Is Loaded and Called

### 3a. `gemini_extractor.py` -- Uses `render_prompts` from `inference.factory`

**File:** `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/medgemma-hackathon/services/protocol-processor-service/src/protocol_processor/tools/gemini_extractor.py`

```python
from inference.factory import render_prompts
# ...
PROMPTS_DIR = Path(__file__).parent.parent / "prompts"
```

Called in two places:

**In `_extract_via_gateway()` (line 142-147):**
```python
system_prompt, user_prompt = render_prompts(
    prompts_dir=PROMPTS_DIR,
    system_template="system.jinja2",
    user_template="user.jinja2",
    prompt_vars={"title": title},

**In `extract_criteria_structured()` (line 221-228):**
```python

    prompts_dir=PROMPTS_DIR,
    system_template="system.jinja2",
    user_template="user.jinja2",
    prompt_vars={
        "title": title,

Both use the `render_prompts` function from `inference.factory` (the shared library), NOT from `prompts/__init__.py`. The `PROMPTS_DIR` is computed as `Path(__file__).parent.parent / "prompts"` which resolves to the same directory. Only `user.jinja2` actually uses the `title` variable; `system.jinja2` has no variables.

Note: `gemini_extractor.py` does NOT import from `protocol_processor.prompts` at all. It bypasses the `__init__.py` helpers entirely.

### 3b. `medgemma_decider.py` -- Uses its own `_render_template`

**File:** `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/medgemma-hackathon/services/protocol-processor-service/src/protocol_processor/tools/medgemma_decider.py`

```python
def _get_prompts_dir() -> "Path":
    from pathlib import Path
    return Path(__file__).parent.parent / "prompts"

def _render_template(template_name: str, **kwargs: Any) -> str:
    from jinja2 import Environment, FileSystemLoader
    prompts_dir = _get_prompts_dir()
    env = Environment(loader=FileSystemLoader(str(prompts_dir)), autoescape=False)
    template = env.get_template(template_name)
    return template.render(**kwargs)
```

Called in `medgemma_decide()` (lines 233-240):
```python
system_prompt = _render_template("grounding_system.jinja2")
evaluate_prompt = _render_template(
    "grounding_evaluate.jinja2",
    entity_text=entity_text,
    entity_type=entity_type,
    criterion_context=criterion_context,
    candidates=candidates,
)
```

Called in `agentic_reasoning_loop()` (lines 401-409):
```python
system_prompt = _render_template("grounding_system.jinja2")
reasoning_prompt = _render_template(
    "grounding_reasoning.jinja2",
    entity_text=entity_text,
    entity_type=entity_type,
    criterion_context=criterion_context,
    previous_query=previous_query,
    attempt=attempt,
)
```

This function can render ANY template name with arbitrary kwargs -- it is more flexible than the `__init__.py` helpers which are hardcoded to `system.jinja2` and `user.jinja2`.

### 3c. `entity_decomposer.py` -- Uses its own `_render_decompose_prompt`

**File:** `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/medgemma-hackathon/services/protocol-processor-service/src/protocol_processor/tools/entity_decomposer.py`

```python
PROMPTS_DIR = Path(__file__).parent.parent / "prompts"

def _render_decompose_prompt(criterion_text: str, category: str | None) -> str:
    env = Environment(loader=FileSystemLoader(str(PROMPTS_DIR)), autoescape=False)
    template = env.get_template("entity_decompose.jinja2")
    return template.render(criterion_text=criterion_text, category=category or "")
```

Called in `decompose_entities_from_criterion()` (line 144):

```

And on retry (line 157):
```python

```

This is more specialized -- hardcodes the template name to `entity_decompose.jinja2` and has a typed signature taking exactly `criterion_text` and `category`.

## 4. Are There TWO Different Template Loading Mechanisms? YES -- Actually THREE.

There are **three** distinct template loading mechanisms in use:

### Mechanism A: `prompts/__init__.py` (`render_system_prompt` / `render_user_prompt`)
- **Location:** `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/medgemma-hackathon/services/protocol-processor-service/src/protocol_processor/prompts/__init__.py`
- **Approach:** `PROMPTS_DIR = Path(__file__).parent` (the prompts package directory itself)
- **Scope:** Hardcoded to `system.jinja2` and `user.jinja2` only
- **Callers:** Nobody in the current codebase actually calls these. They are exported but unused -- they appear to be the original pattern that has since been superseded.

### Mechanism B: `inference.factory.render_prompts()`
- **Location:** `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/medgemma-hackathon/libs/inference/src/inference/factory.py`
- **Approach:** Takes `prompts_dir`, `system_template`, `user_template`, and `prompt_vars` as keyword arguments. Returns a tuple `(system_prompt, user_prompt)`.
- **Creates Environment:** `Environment(loader=FileSystemLoader(str(prompts_dir)))` -- note: no `autoescape=False` explicitly set (defaults to `False` in Jinja2 anyway).
- **Callers:** `gemini_extractor.py` (both the direct Gemini path and the gateway path).

### Mechanism C: Per-file `_render_template` / `_render_decompose_prompt`
- **Locations:** `medgemma_decider.py` and `entity_decomposer.py`
- **Approach:** Each file defines its own private render function with lazy imports of `jinja2`. Both compute `PROMPTS_DIR = Path(__file__).parent.parent / "prompts"`.
- **Creates Environment:** `Environment(loader=FileSystemLoader(str(prompts_dir)), autoescape=False)` -- explicitly sets `autoescape=False`.
- **medgemma_decider._render_template:** Generic -- takes any template name + **kwargs. Used for `grounding_system.jinja2`, `grounding_evaluate.jinja2`, `grounding_reasoning.jinja2`.
- **entity_decomposer._render_decompose_prompt:** Specialized -- hardcodes `entity_decompose.jinja2` with typed parameters.

### Key Differences Between Mechanisms:

| Aspect | Mechanism A (`prompts/__init__`) | Mechanism B (`inference.factory`) | Mechanism C (per-file) |
|---|---|---|---|
| PROMPTS_DIR source | `Path(__file__).parent` | Passed as argument | `Path(__file__).parent.parent / "prompts"` |
| Template names | Hardcoded (system/user) | Parameterized | Hardcoded per function |
| `autoescape` | Explicit `False` | Default (False) | Explicit `False` |
| Caching | None (new env per call) | None (new env per call) | None (new env per call) |
| Return type | Single string | Tuple of (system, user) | Single string |
| Actually used? | **NO** (dead code) | YES (gemini_extractor) | YES (medgemma_decider, entity_decomposer) |

All three mechanisms resolve to the same physical directory. The difference is organizational: Mechanism A is unused, Mechanism B is in a shared library, and Mechanism C is duplicated inline in each tool file.

## 5. All 7 Hardcoded Prompts (Full Text)

### 5a. `field_mapper.py` lines 169-213

**File:** `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/medgemma-hackathon/services/protocol-processor-service/src/protocol_processor/tools/field_mapper.py`

```python
prompt = (
    "<role>Clinical trial protocol analyst. Decompose criterion"
    " into Entity-Relation-Value-Unit field mappings.</role>\n\n"
    "<entity_context>\n"
    f"Medical entity: {grounded_term} {code_context}\n"
    f"Criterion text: {criterion_text}\n"
    "</entity_context>\n\n"
    "<rules>\n"
    "- Extract each discrete measurement, threshold, or condition"
    " as a separate mapping\n"
    "- relation: one of =, !=, >, >=, <, <=, within,"
    " not_in_last, contains, not_contains\n"
    "- Boolean normalization: presence → relation='=', value='True';"
    " absence → relation='!=', value='True'."
    " Never use 'present'/'absent'/'confirmed' as values.\n"
    "- value types: standard {value, unit},"
    " range {min, max, unit}, temporal {duration, unit}\n"
    "- Temporal duration MUST be a plain number."
    ' "within 5 days" → duration="5", unit="days"\n'
    "- Default: if no measurement, use relation='=',"
    ' value={"type":"standard","value":"True","unit":null}\n'
    "- Relative value resolution: when the criterion expresses"
    " a threshold relative to a reference range or normal limit,"
    " you MUST resolve it to an absolute numeric value with a"
    " standard unit. Use your medical knowledge of the entity's"
    " reference range, compute the absolute threshold, and return"
    " only the computed number in the value field and the standard"
    " lab unit in the unit field. Never return the relative"
    " expression as the value.\n"
    "- Extract comparison operators VERBATIM from the criterion text."
    " Never invert the operator direction. If the text says"
    ' ">44", the relation is ">", not "<".\n'
    '- Use "within" ONLY for numeric min/max ranges (e.g., "between'
    ' 18 and 65"). Do NOT use "within" for discrete categorical lists'
    ' like "1, 2, or 3" — use "=" instead.\n'
    '- Negation patterns: "non-", "not ", "no " preceding an entity'
    " indicate ABSENCE. Map to relation='!=' with value='True'."
    " For example, \"non-pregnant\" means Pregnancy relation='!='"
    " value='True'.\n"
    "</rules>\n\n"
    "<anti_patterns>\n"
    "BAD: value='present' → use value='True'\n"
    "BAD: duration='5-days-ago' → use duration='5', unit='days'\n"
    "BAD: value='twice the normal limit' → resolve to absolute\n"
    "</anti_patterns>"
)
```

Uses f-string interpolation with `grounded_term`, `code_context`, and `criterion_text`. Uses XML-style tags (`<role>`, `<entity_context>`, `<rules>`, `<anti_patterns>`).

prompt = (
    "You are a clinical trial protocol analyst. Analyze the logical"
    " structure of this eligibility criterion.\n\n"
    f"Criterion text: {criterion_text}\n\n"
    f"Field mappings (indexed):\n{mappings_text}\n\n"
    "Instructions:\n"
    "- Determine how the field mappings are logically connected\n"
    "- AND = all conditions required simultaneously\n"
    "- OR = any one condition suffices\n"
    "- NOT = negation of a condition\n"
    "- ATOMIC = leaf node referencing a single"
    " field_mapping by index\n"
    "- Return a tree where the root is AND, OR, NOT, or ATOMIC\n"
    "- Every ATOMIC node must have field_mapping_index set to a"
    " valid index (0 to "
    f"{len(field_mappings) - 1})\n"
    "- Each field_mapping index should appear exactly once\n"
)
```

Uses f-string interpolation with `criterion_text`, `mappings_text` (pre-built indexed list), and `len(field_mappings) - 1`.

prompt = (

    "systems and ordinal scales.\n\n"
    "For each entity below, determine if it is a clinical ordinal scoring "
    "system (e.g. ECOG, Karnofsky, NYHA, Child-Pugh, GCS, APACHE II, MELD, "
    "mRS, SOFA, etc.).\n\n"
    "An ordinal scoring system has these characteristics:\n"
    "- Uses discrete numeric grades/scores (not continuous measurements)\n"
    "- Each grade has a specific clinical meaning\n"
    "- The unit is conceptually {score}, not a physical unit\n"
    "- Common in clinical trials for performance status, organ function, "
    "disease severity\n\n"
    "NOT ordinal: lab values with physical units, continuous"
    " measurements, binary conditions, or time-based criteria.\n\n"
    f"Entities to evaluate:\n{entities_text}\n\n"
    "For each entity that IS an ordinal scale, propose:\n"
    "- scale_name: snake_case identifier for YAML config\n"
    "- entity_aliases: alternative names clinicians might use\n"
    "- loinc_code: LOINC code if known\n"
    "- values: list of grades with descriptions\n"
)
```

Uses f-string interpolation with `entities_text` (pre-built indexed entity list). Note: `{score}` on line 72 is a literal curly-brace string (not an f-string interpolation) because the outer string uses regular string concatenation, not f-strings, for that segment.

---

| `structure_builder.py` | None (all inline) | None | 1 (logic detection prompt at lines 142-158) |
| `ordinal_resolver.py` | None (all inline) | None | 1 (ordinal resolution prompt at lines 62-82) |
| `prompts/__init__.py` | Exported but unused | `system.jinja2`, `user.jinja2` | N/A |

     1→# Prompt A/B Testing Harness for Grounding Failures
     2→
     3→## Context
     4→
     5→The full grounding pipeline scores 9/15 exact code match (60%) and has 3 relation mismatches against our golden test data. The 9 failures break into two categories:
     6→
     7→- **6 code mismatches** — MedGemma selects a wrong CUI (subspecific variant, alternate version, or close synonym)
     8→- **3 relation mismatches** — Field mapper generates wrong operator (inverted, wrong class, or missed negation)
     9→
    10→We need a test harness that runs **only the failing entities** with **multiple prompt variants** and produces a comparison report showing which variants fix which failures.
    11→
    12→---
    13→
    14→## Files to Create
    15→
    16→### 1. `tests/e2e/prompt_variants.py` — Variant definitions (pure data)
    17→
    18→Defines all prompt variant content as dicts. Each variant has:
    19→- `name`: short label
    20→- `grounding_system`: replacement system prompt (or `None` for baseline)
    21→- `grounding_evaluate`: replacement Jinja2 evaluate template (or `None`)
    22→- `field_mapper_rules_extra`: extra rules to append to field mapper prompt (or `None`)
    23→
    24→**MedGemma Variants** (for code selection — 4 variants):
    25→
    26→| Variant | Name | Changes |
    27→|---------|------|---------|
    28→| A | `baseline` | No changes — current production prompts |
    29→| B | `specificity_guard` | Add 2 rules to `grounding_system.jinja2`: (1) prefer parent concept over subspecific variants when entity text has no qualifier, (2) prefer primary CUI over alternates when preferred_terms are near-identical |
    30→| C | `context_emphasis` | Modify `grounding_evaluate.jinja2` instructions: add cross-check step — if candidate adds a qualifier not in entity text (e.g. "Juvenile", "Ectopic"), it's too specific; prefer unqualified parent |
    31→| D | `combined` | Both B and C together |
    32→
    33→**Field Mapper Variants** (for relation extraction — 2 variants):
    34→
    35→| Variant | Name | Changes |
    36→|---------|------|---------|
    37→| A | `baseline` | No changes |
    38→| B | `operator_rules` | Append 3 rules: (1) extract comparison operators verbatim from text, never invert; (2) `within` only for numeric min/max ranges, not discrete categorical lists; (3) negation patterns (`non-`, `not `, `no `) map to `relation='!='` |
    39→
    40→### 2. `tests/e2e/run_prompt_variants.py` — Main test harness
    41→
    42→**Failing entities to test** (hardcoded list):
    43→
    44→Code mismatches (tested with MedGemma variants A-D):
    45→| Snippet | Entity | Expected Code |
    46→|---------|--------|---------------|
    47→| 0 | Serum creatinine | C0201975 |
    48→| 1 | eGFR | C0858118 |
    49→| 3:0 | Ankylosing Spondylitis | C0038013 |
    50→| 3:1 | Radiologic examination | C0034693 |
    51→| 4:3 | GBA gene mutation | C4225361 |
    52→| 8:0 | Pregnancy | C0032961 |
    53→
    54→Relation mismatches (tested with field mapper variants A-B):
    55→| Snippet | Entity | Expected Relation |
    56→|---------|--------|-------------------|
    57→| 2:1 | Body Mass Index | `>` |
    58→| 5:0 | ASA physical status | `=` |
    59→| 8:0 | Pregnancy | `!=` |
    60→
    61→**Monkey-patching strategy:**
    62→- MedGemma templates: `unittest.mock.patch("protocol_processor.tools.medgemma_decider._render_template")` with a function that intercepts template names and returns variant content (or falls through to original)
    63→- Field mapper prompt: `unittest.mock.patch("protocol_processor.nodes.ground.generate_field_mappings")` with a wrapper that appends extra rules to the prompt before calling the LLM
    64→
    65→**Execution flow:**
    66→1. Load `test_snippets.json`, extract only failing entities
    67→2. For each MedGemma variant (A-D): patch templates, run 6 code-mismatch entities through `_ground_entity_parallel`, record `selected_code`
    68→3. For each field mapper variant (A-B): patch field mapper, run 3 relation-mismatch entities, record `relation` from first field mapping
    69→4. Print comparison report tables
    70→5. Optionally save JSON results to `tests/e2e/prompt_variant_results/`
    71→
    72→**API call budget:** 6 entities x 4 variants + 3 entities x 2 variants = ~30 grounding calls total
    73→
    74→**Report output format:**
    75→```
    76→CODE SELECTION VARIANTS (MedGemma prompt changes)
    77→═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
    78→Entity                    Expected    baseline  specificity  context  combined
    79→Serum creatinine          C0201975    C0201976  ...          ...      ...
    80→eGFR                      C0858118    C3811844  ...          ...      ...
    81→...
    82→Totals:                               0/6       ?/6          ?/6      ?/6
    83→
    84→RELATION EXTRACTION VARIANTS (Field mapper prompt changes)
    85→═══════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
    86→Entity                    Expected    baseline  operator_rules
    87→Body Mass Index           >           <         ...
    88→ASA physical status       =           within    ...
    89→Pregnancy                 !=          =         ...
    90→Totals:                               0/3       ?/3
    91→```
    92→
    93→---
    94→
    95→## Files to Modify
    96→
    97→None. All production code stays unchanged. The harness uses monkey-patching to inject variant prompts at runtime.
    98→
    99→---
   100→

   103→- `services/protocol-processor-service/src/protocol_processor/tools/medgemma_decider.py` — `_render_template()` at line 39 (patch target for MedGemma variants)
